What the world lacked is a smooth field on a lattice of whole kicks, not the law's form; the next steps registered: a shell every interval or a larger width, the orbit tilted out of the plane, a body of 27 Nodes, and the second reading, a lamp's beam shot at the atom with a phase window (absorption). The experimenter's skill was followed; every line labelled detector or GameBoard; the page with the GameBoard drawn was delivered.

**DECIDED: the catalog of the entities (the owner, 2026-09-20).** The owner's words, translated: "Make sure that every entity the world of physics knows exists in our entity definitions, and that we can also support external ones such as the sun, a planet, a neutron star, so that they can be placed on the GameBoard and things tested." Recorded as a deliverable: a catalog in which every entity physics knows is one row of the law's keys (a family: content per unit, the columns per unit of content with their signs, the lifetime, the phase per Link, the quantum, the table; a measured event: amount, momentum, fixed, span, phase_by_momentum, a lamp; a composite: the bound set and the detector's set that sees it as one), the fundamental ones (the leptons, the neutrinos, the quarks, the photon, the W and Z, the gluon, the Higgs), the composites (the nucleons, the nuclei, the atoms), the external things (the sun and the stars, a planet, a neutron star, a white dwarf, a black hole, a lamp, a mirror, a wall and a slit, a screen, a detector), each with a world file that places it on the GameBoard and parses and runs, and an honest gap list: every entity the law cannot yet place, with the key it would need (colour and confinement, the Higgs, a black hole beyond the integer bound or as a body whose clock the crowd stops, molecules). The entity definitions document is the owner of the catalog; the register tests things on them.

**The physicist's design of the strong force (2026-09-20, scratchpad/strong/DESIGN.md, flagged, for the owner's decisions).** Built on the lifetime (a), the one mechanism (a signed inner product over named columns, gravity the built-in column of value 1 and sign minus, `charge` sign plus, `strong` a declared column of sign minus, no code path for the strong force) and "our laws are on the GameBoard, the detector sees other laws". The reach of a lifetime L, measured on the engine's flight table: L = 1 the six neighbours; L = 2 also the face diagonals; L = 3 the cube diagonals and two Links; a nucleus with a bond of tensile strength needs L >= 3. The strong reading is the electric reading's own 1 / r^2 with the opposite sign and a cut, a square well, not Yukawa's exponential: a stated difference from nature. The deuteron binds at one Link and no strong ray reaches three; two protons bind at one Link iff G^2 + M^2 > Q^2, the owner's sigma^2 > rho^2 - 1 exactly. The alpha: the square p n / n p is sheared (the p-p diagonal bond weaker than the n-n one by exactly Q^2 x U_d per row) and under the contact rule ejects a proton, while the line p n n p holds exactly (no step in 3000 intervals): on the lattice the alpha candidate is the line, and series I decides. He-5 and Li-5 bind at L >= 2: the law has no exclusion principle, a known difference from nature. Decay is deterministic, one interval per world, no spread across the 48 orientations. No mass defect in the content; the one reading of a binding energy is the clock's count (the bound clock 10.9 % slower at one Link). The proton for the register is a measured event holding 1836 of a charged family and one unit of a strong family (`nuclear`, strong 10000, lifetime 3), since a lifetime is a key of a family of rays and the electric rays live forever: a measured event holds content of several families, which the engine's books already do (D-1: the charge of a measured event is the rational sum over what it holds). THE CONTACT PROBLEM, measured: the p-n pair at one Link takes 1836 x 1839 x 3008 per interval each way with the step refused every time, the labels reaching the integer bound after 454 million intervals (15 million with sigma). The rule designed: the refused step is a read of the body as a paid ray (kappa = 1): the axis component of the momentum joins the occupant's and leaves the body's, a `contact` record; the pair's sum unchanged, each label bounded by one interval's push; the books, LOCALITY-1 (one Link), the bijection (the steps are the frame's) and no register hold; for the width-3 electron the component goes to the occupants of the destination set apportioned by content. Collinear clusters hold and planar ones shear under it. Open for the owner: (1) the contact rule; without it every bound pair's labels grow without bound and series I cannot be registered; (2) L >= 3 for the strong family of the register; (3) the alpha as the line on the lattice, a finding to be tested, not a decision.

**Series G landed: the Hubble diagram behind the detector (2026-09-20, the run).** Twenty-four sources thrown from the centre of a 301^3 cube at 0.05 c to 0.6 c, a family each, chains of four on the six axes, releasing toward the centre with their clock's phase; a detector of one Node at the centre reading `wave` with `reads: "age"`; six masses at one Link as the mass inside; two crowds (coasting and pushing) and the two clocks (scalar and age); the expectation written first. The detector reads the linear Hubble law by itself: z = v / c to 0.003 for every source of the coasting throw and H t_0 = 1.03, the Hubble time the age of the throw; the reading's formula 1 + z = (1 + k)(1 + v / c) within 2 % in 288 of 288, the Doppler of the throw times the emitter's clock and nothing else. At large distance the detector's curve falls below the coasting form of its own near fit in every run, so of the three forms the one observed today, q = -0.55, is the nearest in ten of twelve windows, against the expectation that it be outside everywhere: the detector sees the signature of an accelerating recession while nothing accelerates on the GameBoard (the coasting momenta within 3 %, the pushing ones falling 4 to 32 %). The resemblance comes from what the detector cannot see: in the coasting throw the sources' initial distances (each reads the Milne form shifted by its start, the far ones started three times farther out), in the pushing crowd the emitters' clocks (the inner sources sit in the thickest crowd and their clocks run slowest, k up to 0.07, reddening the near part and inflating the near fit's H; with the clock removed the Doppler part reads the deceleration, H t_0 < 1 in every window). With H fitted per form the three forms agree within 0.005 in z: at this precision the shape does not tell q = +0.5 from 0 from -0.55. The age clock bends nothing beyond the grain. 22 pinned readings inside, 26 outside, none moved. What the law lacked, registered: a straight throw off the axes (the step rule loses a coinciding step), a three-dimensional gravity of a crowd of points (a beam never dilutes, a fan misses the Nodes off its lines), a source that releases along its own motion without taking its row home, emitters' clocks that count only their own crowd, and a step rule whose whole part does not cluster under a changing momentum. The experimenter's skill followed; the page with the GameBoard drawn delivered.

**"The page does not run: one cannot see it move by time" (the owner, 2026-09-20).** On the page of series G. Recorded as an addition to the page contract: the moving picture is played inside the page by a frame player with a time control (play and pause, a slider over the intervals, the interval shown), the GIF kept as a link, so that the run is seen moving by time in every viewer; the pages of series G and H were rebuilt so and republished at the same links.

**DECIDED: the contact rule, generic through the table; the strong force goes to implementation (the owner, 2026-09-20, "is the contact rule the generic one?", then "go").** The contact rule is the one mechanism of a paid arrival applied to a body: a body that steps into a Node held by another measured event is an arrival at the occupant, and the occupant's table entry for the body's family decides as for any arrival: `measure` hands the body's momentum component on that axis to the occupant (the designed rule: the pair's sum unchanged, each label bounded by one interval's push, a `contact` record), `rerelease` returns it (the body bounces, the occupant takes twice the component, the sum unchanged), the default derived from the keys as for every table; no separate rule, no family name, no formula; one Link, the steps the frame's, nothing at a Node, the books balanced. With the owner's "go" the implementation of the strong force starts on the physicist's design: the columns as the one signed inner product (gravity the built-in column of value 1 and sign minus, `charge` the shorthand for the electric column of sign plus, a declared column with its sign for the strong force, no code path for any force), the lifetime L as a family key on the event's own age with the escape booked as a click on the border `lifetime`, a measured event holding content of several families with its charge the rational sum over what it holds, the contact through the table, the model identity in the run's record, the tests (a) to (f) with the design's integers, the migration note, and series I, the nucleus (the deuteron at one and three Links, the alpha as the line and the square, He-5, Z_max, the decay across orientations), with its page. The mathematician's verification of the columns form (bit-exactness against every registered integer) runs beside it and is a condition of the merge.

**The mathematician's verdict on the columns: correct and working (2026-09-20, scratchpad/columns/COLUMNS.md).** The generic form, push = sum over the columns of epsilon_c x sign(V n_A n_B) x by_clock(age_A, |V n_A n_B M_A|, d_A d_B), equals today's push integer by integer: the gravity column (1, 1) with the sign minus is exact because a whole division by 1 is the identity, the electric column is today's very call, and the three bounds coincide; 611,600 cases equal on grids and at the register's scale; replayed through the engine on 46 worlds (series 7, C, D, E, Bell and H), 11,945 pushes compared and 11,945 equal, 4,776 read records recomputed from the record alone and equal. The bounds rule: each column floored separately (summing then flooring differs, by at most k - 1), the product tested by division before it is formed, the partial sum bounded after each column (the law's spare bit makes the sum of two bounded terms safe), the parser's static budget per ordered pair of families; the host cost k products per arriving group, nothing per ray. Merging a strong column into gravity is not integer-neutral for unlike fractional values, so the columns stay separate. The third law by the rays unchanged. The world-file form: `charge` as it is, `"columns": {"strong": {"value": [n, d], "sign": -1}}` per family, gravity implicit, a declared gravity refused; all 66 example worlds parse unchanged. The implementation of the strong force started on the two designs.

**The physicist's design of the weak force and the list of the law's own predictions (2026-09-20, scratchpad/weak/WEAK.md and PREDICTIONS.md, flagged, for the owner's decisions).** The weak force in the law's terms: one table rule `become` (a measured event becomes another family's event and releases the rest as rows born as a re-release is, the products paid from what it holds, the remainder moved to the new family, a `became` line per family in the books, the content exact, the charge conserved, the recoil over all products), one function with two triggers: the clock (the key `become: {at, into, products, crowd}` fired at the self-creation whose clock reaches `at`; `crowd` an optional gate that fires only while the count the clock read is below it, the law's form of the condition that makes a bound neutron stable) and the click (a table entry with a phase window: the capture, and a bath that gives an exponential survival deterministically); both admissible with no draw on the GameBoard. The W a paid family with lifetime 1 and no column (it holds nothing; measured, its push is its label); L = 0 is no family at all, Fermi's contact form; no Z family (the neutral current a windowed re-release). Identity `weak-v1`. The neutrino: today's phase window is the half circle whatever its centre (320 of 640 arrivals click); a table-entry key `phase_width` (N / 2 by default, bit-identical) makes the admitted fraction w / N exactly when the source's stride is coprime to N (w = 1: 10 of 640; at N = 4096, 10 of 40960, the smallest one gate gives; nature's 10^-17 per nucleon needs about three such gates compounded); the cross-section is flat in energy, a plain disagreement with nature's linear rise. Law changes the design needs, for the owner: D-1, a paid family may declare a whole charge per unit of amount on the charge line only (without it the neutron's products are refused); the key `phase_width`. A finding: the engine's clock ticks in an interval in which the body steps, while TERMINOLOGY says a transfer is not a self-creation; the two read a moving body's clock differently (no slowing against 1 - v, first order, Manhattan-anisotropic), neither gives nature's gamma (the muon in flight); series J4 would tell them apart; not changed, an unresolved contradiction to be decided by the owner. Parity is exact on the GameBoard; a hand needs an axial record and a right-hand rule in `become` (`hand-v1`), restricts that family to the 24 proper rotations, breaks no bijection, only mirror equivariance; not needed for any series J reading. The unification in two answers: on the GameBoard one mechanism (a family with a lifetime and columns; the reader's table decides: `measure` the push, `become` the transformation; a W-like family may do both); in the detector nothing in the readings changes with energy (window, lifetime and push all flat), so no scale where the weak meets the electric, and nature's broken symmetry, the Higgs, the W and Z masses fixing the scale, the propagator's rise, the running couplings and V-A have no counterpart. Verdict: admissible; the order recommended: the neutrino first with no law change (`phase_width`, series J2), then `become` (series J1, J3), then the W world, then the hand if wanted; every disagreement registered as the limit, never tuned. PREDICTIONS.md, 23 entries, the five strongest: (1) M / r on the age clock and M / r^2 on the push from the same rays, agrees in form (series E; the rate 1 / (1 + k) never reaches zero: no horizon); (2) light is neither bent nor delayed by a mass, since no rule lets a ray in transit read the crowd, a plain disagreement (1.75 arcseconds, Shapiro); (3) the field of a moving body points to its retarded position (the push along the arriving u_d, nothing of the emitter's velocity on the record), an aberration at order v / c that decays two free bodies' orbit within a few turns, a strong disagreement (the binary pulsar); (4) a bound body's active gravitational flux is slowed by its count, M / (1 + k), while its inertia is not, a Nordtvedt-like effect of 10 to 50 % at the register's suspensions against nature's below 10^-4, a disagreement; (5) the wave only in the detector: fringes need two rays of one number in one interval, so a screen counting single clicks shows the fan's spots, against the single-particle build-up of fringes (Tonomura), the test being fringe contrast against source rate. Also: no mass defect, the flat neutrino cross-section, parity exact, a step survival and a line beta spectrum, neutrinos that gravitate but carry no content, bodies that can outrun light, no radiation from accelerated charges (agrees for the atom, disagrees for bremsstrahlung), Bell S = 2, the Hubble law linear near.

**DECIDED: the law of the ray is the Beam Law (the owner, 2026-09-20: "worth changing the name of the ray law to Beam Law, no?").** Yes: the thing was named NatureBeam by the owner and "there is no ray, there is only an event", so the law that governs the beam, the record of an event in transit, is the Beam Law. Done in the rename in flight: docs/RAY_LAW.md becomes docs/BEAM_LAW.md, "the law of the ray" becomes "the Beam Law" in every document, skill and docstring, the law's identity `rays-v1` becomes `beam-v1` and the world key `"law": "rays"` becomes `"law": "beam"` with every world file of the repository rewritten by the migration tool and the old value refused naming the migration; the run records of the registered runs stay historical ("rays-v1 is beam-v1, the same law"); the hypothesis identities are untouched; the word "ray" stays allowed in prose as the informal name of the beam. The quotations of this document keep their wording.

The owner's condition, taken as the closing gate of the whole stack before it goes to main: the physics-rule reviewer on every changed rule (the columns, the lifetime, the contact, the held content, `become`, `phase_width`, D-1) and the architect's genericity probe (relabel and permute the families and the columns of the registered worlds, every reading equal) on the merged tree, both read-only, both with a written verdict; a blocking finding stops the merge.

**Issues #359 and #360 of the owner's other session, read against the branch (2026-09-20).** #360, the momentum per quantum scaling with |D|: resolved on the branch by the label along the unit vector at Q = 64 (the decision of 2026-09-19); the minor item, the world's escaped momentum written into every family's entry of run.json, queued for the next engine change. #359, the click phase-blind: on the branch a detector is a set with one record and under the default reading `wave` the click is phase-aware within an interval (the window reads the pointer's phase, a zero pointer passes, the record is the pointer's square), but the threshold still reads the amount, not the pointer's square (the issue's step A in full), and nothing persists between intervals (its step B, a memory of two integers on the detector, the external thing, so that counts read |sum of phasors|^2 one ray at a time, Born-like and deterministic, no Bell violation, and rays that cancel still pass). Open for the owner: step A, a small generic change (under `wave` the threshold reads what the reading is, the pointer's square in units of one ray: 1, 4, 0; under `beam` the amount), recommended; step B, a change of law with its own identity, to be decided after A10 at a low rate reports whether fringes build up one click at a time.

**DECIDED: issue #359, step A yes, step B no (the owner, 2026-09-20).** Step A: under the reading `wave` a detector set's threshold reads what the reading is, the square of the coherent pointer of the arrivals in units of one ray (one ray 1, two in phase 4, two opposite 0), and under `beam` the amount as today; no memory, the interval's arrivals only; a small generic change of the click, with its test, in the next engine change on the branch. Step B, the pointer kept between intervals, is refused, in the owner's reading: "B is a detector parameter that is not known to work in the real world": nature's detectors keep no phase between arrivals (a plate or a CCD does not), the single-particle fringes of Tonomura come from the particle, not the screen, so B would reproduce the counts by a mechanism that does not exist, a formula placed in the detector against the rule that the detector only reads; from a parameter on the detector any count can be had, so it is a patch, not a prediction. If A10 at a low rate shows no build-up one click at a time, that is registered as the law's limit (the wave in the crowd, not in the single event); the honest route to single-particle fringes, if ever, is on the GameBoard, the event carrying something of the wave, which the Beam Law removed on purpose: a law question for later, not a patch.

**DECIDED: every external thing brought from the real world into the GameBoard carries its uncertainty like the detector; on the GameBoard it is certain (the owner, 2026-09-20: "verify that this is the situation, for an emitter and the like").** Verified on the engine of the branch: an emitter (a lamp, a source, a star) is a measured event like the detector and declares the same widths on the world's side, its set (`span`, a body on a block of Nodes with one record; a lamp of span [1, 1, 3] releases over its three Nodes, 262146 + 262146 + 262140 = the one-Node release of 786432, apportioned whole by its age) and its phase window (`phase_window`, "the declared width of a detector and of an emitter", the owner's decision of 2026-09-19), while on the GameBoard every release is a definite event at a Node, in an interval, on a direction, with a phase, made by the clock and the fan with no draw. So the symmetry holds: the world knows an emitter as its set and its window, as it knows a detector, and which Node released or which Node clicked is not information the world has; the GameBoard knows both. The same for a mirror (a re-release, definite), a wall, a mass, a probe. Added to the catalog's rows: the widths on the world's side of every external thing and the statement that its events on the GameBoard are definite.

**DECIDED: a mathematician on the formulas and the genericity (the owner, 2026-09-20: "maybe run an agent of formulas and genericity, to check how everything can be defined in one beautiful formula, or a few, or in general to think what can be unified with us beautifully").** Sent as a read-only task: the law as it stands on the branch (the event's record; the family's keys: the columns with their signs, the lifetime, the phase per Link, the quantum, the table; the one interval: the flight, the collision, the table on the arrivals, the one push, the one clock, the step, the click; the detector's reading as the moments and the pointer) written as the smallest set of formulas that reproduces every registered integer, with every place where two things are the same primitive named (the clock and the lifetime, an age against a key by whole division; the threshold, the window and the record as readings of the one pointer; the push and the reading as the same moments; the step rule and the owed count), what one formula would cost if it changed an integer, and what stays separate and why.

**"In the experiments where one sees an electron around a nucleus, is it in fact events? Does the electron split, or does something act on it? Because everything on the board is events" (the owner, 2026-09-20; the orchestrator's answer, flagged).** Events, and nothing splits. On the GameBoard the electron is one measured event, a body on its set of Nodes with one record: at every interval it is at one place, whole, and it steps whole; what it makes is other events, the free rays it releases from its Node on its fan, carrying its phase and no content (so releasing costs it nothing), and the event it becomes under a `become` entry when the weak force comes. What acts on it is only what arrives at its set: the proton's events, which its table reads as the push; the turn of its own phase by its own steps; its own clock. Nothing acts from afar. What the experiments "see", the orbital, the cloud, is a detector's reading: the sum in one record of the rays the electron released from every place it passed over many intervals, or of a beam re-released off it; a picture of where it was when each ray left, never a thing on the GameBoard. And a detector whose set covers three Nodes reads "one electron, on any of the three", the owner's own principle of sensitivity, while the GameBoard knows which. So the electron does not spread: its releases do, and a set sums them.

The quotations of this document keep their wording and this document's own mention of RAY_LAW stays as history.

**The catalog of the entities landed (2026-09-20, docs/ENTITY_CATALOG.md, examples/events/catalog, the test).** Every entity physics knows as one row of the law's keys: nine fundamental rows (the electron; the muon and tau; the three neutrinos; the six quarks; the photon; the W and Z; the gluon; the Higgs; the graviton as what the law has instead, gravity the universal column), eight composites (the proton, the neutron, the deuteron, the alpha, light nuclei, hydrogen, helium, a molecule), seventeen external things (the sun and a star, a planet, a moon, a neutron star, a white dwarf, a black hole, a galaxy, a comet, a lamp, a laser, a mirror, a wall, a slit, a screen and detector, a clock, a probe, dark matter), each external one with its widths on the world's side and its definite events on the GameBoard; sixteen rows wait for the keys in flight (the strong column, the lifetime, the held content, the contact, `become`, `phase_width`, D-1, the hand, the wave threshold); a gap list of eighteen. Four worlds place the external things and run with the books balanced: the sun on a set of three with a planet on a set of nine curving inward and a screen reading its reflected light; a neutron star of eight neutrons at one Link with probes, one reading the age; a laser, a mirror, a slit and a screen; two clocks of 27 Nodes beside a mass, both owing counts. The judgements for the owner: a black hole cannot be placed, and the law's dense body is the opposite of one, dark and weightless in the limit (a measured event's field is its releases and the crowd's count postpones them without end; no horizon, the rate 1 / (1 + k) never zero; the reading: its light thins out and a probe's count falls back toward the free rate); a star is today two measured events at adjacent Nodes (a paid family carries no charge) until the held content lets a lamp release from a body; light is pulled and bent by nothing until series K says otherwise; dark matter is placeable today (a free family of charge 0 that passes light), dark energy is not in the law; a body's width is a width of its push (about w times one Node's flux), so wide bodies need a large `width` to orbit slowly.

Two decisions: (4) the memory at the detector, refused by the owner, and the falsified prediction of additive single-click counts written into HYPOTHESES section 11 so that it can fail, with A10 at a low rate as its measurement; (5) Bell in one sentence, written into HYPOTHESES section 11: the model predicts S = 2, the loophole-free experiments measured 2.4 to 2.7, the model fails here by choice for locality; the audit's measurement of the detection loophole (a narrow window with `pass`: S = 4 on the counted coincidences at 72 % efficiency, S over all pairs below 2, the experiments before 2015 reproduced and the loophole-free ones refuting) recorded as a precise negative result; postulate 22's shared number not restored. Verified sound by the same audit, no action: the isotropic speed, Gauss exact through every ring and shell, the third law and superposition exact, the books closed with `pass` at every threshold, the engine's cost independent of the GameBoard's size.

**The audit's two remarks on the record, adopted (issue #361, 2026-09-20).** (1) Beside THE PRINCIPLE: a wave is the sum of the events reaching one set in one interval, so the principle holds for a crowd and is the model's limit for a single quantum per interval (written next to it above). (2) Bell, sharpened in HYPOTHESES section 11: nature satisfies no-signalling exactly and still reaches S = 2.83, so what the model keeps is stronger than what nature keeps: the local determination of every outcome by the arriving event and the setting, which Bell's theorem, not no-signalling, is what S > 2 refutes; no-signalling, which nature does keep, the model keeps as well, measured exact in A2. The choice recorded is therefore "outcome determination against nature's correlations", not "locality against non-locality". (3) On state: none of the day's changes are on main yet, so numbers measured on main are not the model's until the merge.

**The mathematician on the formulas: the Beam Law is two formulas and one table, composed (2026-09-20, scratchpad/formula/ONE_FORMULA.md, flagged).** The one sentence: the GameBoard's state is a multiset of integer records and the interval is one fixed map on it, the composition frame, walk, collision, tables, self-creations, merge and the frame's tail, in which every reading is a moment M_k = sum of w x u^(k) of the records at a set, every rate is the whole part off an age, floor((a + 1) r) - floor(a r), the collision a table permutation inside the class (M_0, M_1), and the click the one projection. Three primitives: the moment, the floor difference (`by_clock`) and the class permutation; plus the border and the constants (u_d and T_d by integer root, the cosines and sines by the fixed-point series). Proved the same primitive, each verified numerically: the clock, the lifetime, `become at` and the age bound are an age against a key by `by_clock(a, 1, key)`; the threshold, the window, the record and the phase returned are |P|^2 and arg P of one pointer; the push and the clock's count are M_1 and M_0 of one moment table over two nested sets (the presence and the admitted arrivals: the one reading set is one function on two subsets, stated honestly); the step rule and the owed count the same `by_clock` at the same age; the flight and the label the same u_d (the position after tau intervals within 1.26 % of tau u_d / (Q sqrt 3) over 1730 directions); the collision class exactly (crowd, M_0, M_1) of the singles, equivariant under O_h while the map is not; the columns bit-exact; the five table values a chain by how many fields the reader takes; and, found: the turn is the release at rate [1, K], and the pointer equals the one reading on the cosines and sines with weight 32 x amount bit for bit. Theorems: the books as invariants of the map; the bijection up to the click; the 48 symmetries with every rule classed invariant, equivariant or handed (handed: the collision's sorted order, the Bresenham tie, the x before y before z step order, `age mod n`, the last-in-set phase); time reversal; the scale Q exact in clocks and labels and a resolution in the flight; genericity as a theorem, families reversed and events renumbered on three worlds through the engine with no differing tick. Three unifications worth doing, all safe, no registered integer moves: (1) the pointer as the first moment of the one reading over the circle, one call site; (2) every age against a key as the one `by_clock`, the turn declared as the release at [1, K], which makes K a rational for free; (3) the presence, the age moment and the push as one moment-table call over one "set" object shared by a body and a detector (the data only; making a detector one measured event would re-register A10 and the slit worlds, the owner's decision). One for the owner: the electric column is floored at the age after the frame's advance while every other rate uses the clock age (note 20); unifying costs one fractional pin and moves no registered series. Refused: the quantum as a column (the paid push is a conservation), N = Q (a coincidence of defaults), any phase-reading collision or inter-interval pointer memory. What resists one formula: the collision table (an order, not an arithmetic), the click (a projection), the tie-breaks.

**DECIDED: the four unifications of the formulas, after the strong force lands (the owner, 2026-09-20, "go on all four after the strong one").** (1) The pointer as the first moment of the one reading over the circle, one call site, with the exact fallback beyond 2^48 so the record stays never refused; (2) every age against a key as the one `by_clock` (the lifetime, `become at`, the age bound as `by_clock(a, 1, key)`) and the turn declared as the release at rate [1, K], which makes K a rational pair like the release rate with no new rule; (3) the presence, the age moment and the push as one moment-table call over one "set" object shared by a body and a detector, the data only, a detector staying a detector; (4) the electric column floored at the clock age like every other rate, the one exception of note 20 removed, at the cost of one fractional pin in the push tests and no registered series moving. Every logic once, to the letter; the full suite and the replay of the register are the proof that the law is the same.

**Series K and A10 at a low rate landed: two predictions of the law measured against nature (2026-09-20, the runs).** Series K, light beside a mass: a narrow beam of a paid family past a fixed mass of 2^12 on series E's fan at the impact distance 6, at twice the mass, and at 3, read at a screen of 1681 one-Node `wave` pixels with the age on every click, against a control without the mass; the expectation written first from the collision's rule (it acts per family and per number and content, so a beam's ray and a mass's ray never enter one slot state: a mean deflection of exactly zero because the rule does not act). Measured: the centroid's deflection 0.000 pixels in y and z, the delay 0.00 intervals (the mean age 89.40 in every world), the count 1.0000 of the control, the phase rate 8.000, nothing taken by the mass, while a third of the beam's rows shared a Node with a ray of the crowd every interval; 16 readings inside, 0 outside, at a crowd where nature would capture the beam (2 G M / c^2 of 137 to 273 Links against b = 6). Light is neither bent nor delayed beside a mass in this law, exactly: the physicist's prediction 2 registered as measured, a plain disagreement with nature. What the law lacked: a rule by which an event in transit reads the crowd at the Node it enters, removed on purpose on 2026-09-19; the series E clock reads the crowd on the emitter and the reader, never on the light between them. A10 at a low rate: the registered w27 opening at the lamps' rates 47, 8 and 1 (6.7, 1.1 and 0.14 rays per pixel per interval, the same 172,000 clicks in each window). Measured: the narrowing of the coherent record against the count 0.307, 0.052, -0.017; the share of clicks in cells with two or more rays 100 %, 65 %, 12 %; at the lowest rate the record's spread is the count's, no lobe, and the record is the count plus the cross terms of the rare coincidences of the synchronized comb (spikes of 0.43 to 1.20 of the count; the pinned R / I at the peak 1.20 against 1 +- 0.02, outside as pinned). Fringes do not build up one click at a time in this law: the physicist's prediction 5 registered as measured, a plain disagreement with nature and, by the owner's decision on #359, the law's limit: the wave is in the crowd, not in the single event. A follow-up without a change of law: the opening's fans rotated against one another and a rate of one ray on the whole screen per interval. The experimenter's skill followed; the pages with the GameBoard drawn and the frame player delivered.

**DECIDED: the generalized collision, an event in transit reads the crowd reversibly (the owner, 2026-09-20, "go for it").** The road there, in the owner's questions and the answers: "does this solve many things?" (it gives light its gravity and the optics of a medium, and opens the pilot route to single-click fringes; it does not touch the aberration, the Nordtvedt-like effect, the horizon, the mass defect or Bell); "in a mass there is a push and a ray, no?" (yes: a measured event both releases events and reads the push of the events that arrive; an event in transit today only is the release and reads nothing, the one asymmetry of the law); and the owner's correction: "but if the board goes back in time, what pushed was there, no?" Right: reversibility is not lost if the rule is a bijection of everything at the Node together, as the collision table already is (verified on 3^8 states), because on the reversed GameBoard the crowd is back at that Node in that interval and the inverse step reads the same flow. So the reversible form of a beam reading the crowd is a generalized collision: at a meeting of a light event with a mass's event the light turns toward the mass by delta and the mass's event turns away by delta, the momentum of the meeting conserved, the rule a permutation of the joint state (invertible), the sign toward the mass from the sign of the gravity column as in the push on a body; the reading of the push done as an exchange, not as a memory; what a body does today is the same with the crowd standing still. Not a third law but an extension of the collision table across families with a sign and conservation, and a proof of bijectivity like the 3^8 one. Sent to the physicist and the mathematician as a joint read-only design: the table, the proof, the condition that every series of bodies (C, 7, D, E, G, I) keeps every integer, and what series K and A10 at a low rate would read under it, the pilot route (a lamp's free family as the wave and rare paid quanta that meet it) as an appendix with a prototype.

**Issue #362, entanglement as a detector set spanning both arms, for the owner's decision (2026-09-20, the owner's other session).** The idea, from the owner's own statements (every event definite on the GameBoard, the external things carrying the world's uncertainty, a detector a set with one record): one set holding Alice's and Bob's Nodes, the correlation made in the reading and not carried by the rays. Measured on the branch: the set alone changes nothing (four A2 worlds, the four counters as one set: S = 2.05; each Node still decides by its own window on the common phase). What Bell's theorem fixes: two detectors that decide separately cannot exceed 2, whatever they shared; to reach 2.83 the set must decide jointly, one outcome per pair drawn once with both windows known, which is by definition a detector that is not local; and the record cannot be the phase the rays carry (nature's cos^2 depends on the settings' difference alone), so it would be a new object written at birth, one record for two places, outside the GameBoard: postulate 22's registry under another name, hollow if taken alone (the GameBoard delivering tagged rays, the record doing the physics), reversing three recorded decisions (no draw, no memory at the detector, no answer at a distance). The recommendation there, adopted here: decide the amplitude question once, for the single-click fringes and for Bell together, after the pilot route of the generalized collision is known; the design in flight is asked to state what a local pilot gives for a pair (expected: S at most 2, since Bohm's guiding wave that reaches 2.83 is one object for two particles, which no local rule of the GameBoard is). Until then the state stands: S = 2 as the law's limit in HYPOTHESES 11. Nothing of this blocks the merge; the only thing that depends on it is what a paper claims, exact classical physics on an integer lattice with a declared quantum limit, or quantum mechanics on one.

**DECIDED: the API of the GameBoard is an emitter in and a detector out (the owner, 2026-09-20).** The owner's words, translated: "Note that when you intervene in the GameBoard it requires a detector on the GameBoard or an emitter; that is the API." Recorded as the interface rule of the model (ARCHITECTURE, the experimenter's skill): every way the world touches the GameBoard is one of its external things, both measured events with declared widths: input through an emitter (a measured event that releases events by its clock and its table: a lamp, a source, a star, a mirror), output through a detector (a set with one record, its clicks the only reading reality has); the world file's declarations are the state at interval 0; a host reading of the dense arrays is a diagnostic or a picture, never a measurement; no tool, test or agent sets an event at a Node during a run; a rule that acts on an event in transit (the collision, the generalized collision in design) is a meeting of events on the GameBoard, not an intervention. The pilot route obeys it: the wave is what an emitter releases and the quantum is what it releases, and the fringes are what a detector counts.

**DECIDED: #362 waits for the pilot (the owner, 2026-09-20, "waiting for the pilot; update me when there is a result").** After Bell explained in plain terms (the gloves: an answer packed in each envelope in advance gives S at most 2, which is the model today with the phase as the glove and the window as the opening; nature's 2.83 with no message means the answers are not in the envelopes but made at the question, coordinated by one thing of the pair that has no place; Bell forbids the third way, not the second), and the four things 2.83 would need (the pair's identity, in place; a draw, one per pair; the record kept from the first click to the second; a joint reading rule with the cosine from an amplitude kept in the record), the owner defers the decision on the amplitude to the result of the generalized collision's pilot appendix: if the pilot gives something of the wave on the GameBoard, the decision is taken once for the single-click fringes and for Bell together; if not, #362 is decided on its own. Nothing in flight is blocked.

**"In the end everything comes down to a transformation between the GameBoard and the world we experience" (the owner, 2026-09-20).** Recorded as the standing statement above the day's principles, with the reading, flagged: the GameBoard is events only, definite and reversible up to the click; the world we experience is what the external things read of them, and every reading is one map, the moments of the events that reach a set in one interval (the count, the flow, the tensor, the age moment) and the coherent pointer, made by an emitter's declared widths on the way in and a detector's set on the way out. That one map is the transformation: the wave, the uncertainty, the clock's slowing, the redshift, Bohr's lines, the Hubble law are its forms, and nature's laws are what it gives. The generalized collision extends the same map into the middle of a flight (an event reading what it meets), and the one open question, #362, is whether the transformation needs anything of the pair that the GameBoard's events do not carry.

**QUEUED: #363, Bell with the choosers on the GameBoard (opened 2026-09-20; a measurement, not a change of law).** In A2 the counters' windows are written in the world file, a hand from outside the universe, so S = 2 is established only given a free choice from outside, and a fully deterministic GameBoard is open to the superdeterminism reading (HYPOTHESES 11). #363 measures instead of arguing: A2 as registered, each counter's window read from the phase of a ray arriving from a third source (Alice) and a fourth (Bob), far from each other and from the pair's lamp, with no shared clock and no common release, so the settings change per pair and are decided by GameBoard events. The prediction is S = 2: the law does not correlate things that never met, and the S = 2 of HYPOTHESES 11 becomes a result about the law rather than about the file. S not 2 would mean the law itself correlates the sources, a mechanism to find before anything else. Cost: one world option, a counter's window read from an arriving event's phase (a `reads` key for the window, as the clock and the push already select their component of the one reading); the A2 reader as is. This does not give 2.83; entanglement stays with #362. Boss decision: queued after the strong-force merge, run by the experimenter, no owner decision needed (an experiment within the API rule: the third and fourth sources are emitters, the counters are detectors).

**RECORDED: what the model claims today, stated by the auditor on #361 (2026-09-20).** "Exact classical physics on an integer lattice, with the quantum limit declared and measured": the fields, the movement, the clock, the redshift and the Hubble law are exact and local on the GameBoard; the single-click fringes and Bell's 2.83 are the declared limit, measured in A10 low rate and A2 and stated in HYPOTHESES 11 so they can fail. The sentence changes only if the pilot route of the generalized collision gives something of the wave on the GameBoard (#362 waits for that result).

**"There is no outside" (the owner, in the other session, 2026-09-20, on #363: "this is right because there is nothing external to the GameBoard; all of reality is represented on the GameBoard, according to the model").** Recorded with its reading, flagged as the orchestrator's: everything that affects an outcome is an event on the GameBoard; the world file is an initial state, not a hand that keeps intervening; once the run starts, everything follows from the law alone. Read together with the API rule above (an emitter in, a detector out): the emitter and the detector are the border where the world's uncertainty meets the GameBoard's certainty, declared in the initial state, and a human puts nothing on the GameBoard during a run (the experimenter's skill). A counter's window written in the file is a setting fixed at the start, legitimate as an initial state but not a chooser; #363 makes the chooser an event (the window read from the phase of a ray from a distant source), so that "is the model superdeterministic" becomes a numerical question about the law. The one condition: the chooser of the initial state stays outside, so the principle holds for a generic initial state, one not built to correlate; #363's control 3 (three lamps fed from one clock must give S different from 2) shows that the measurement sees a correlation built in on purpose. If #363 reads S = 2 with every E on the triangle, the model claims full determinism without superdeterminism, a line added to what the model claims (#361) once measured, not before.

**RECORDED: the six issues of the old engine, and where each target lives (2026-09-20).** The old engine, its draws and its detector candidates were deleted (the shared quantum resource on 2026-09-17, the one engine on 2026-09-19; docs/MIGRATION.md). #342 (physical detector Nodes with causal readout and explicit information ownership): its implementation milestone closed with PRs #344 and #346 on 2026-09-19; under the Beam Law the detector is a `DetectorSet` (a set of Nodes with one record, a threshold, a window and a declared reading, the click the only one-way border), an external thing by the API rule, so its requirement of a shared result formed by local propagation is superseded by the owner's decisions that the detector's reading is one map over the set and that the uncertainty is the detector's, not the GameBoard's; its open scientific targets (Heisenberg, Born, Bell) live in HYPOTHESES 11 and the A10 and A2 registrations; closed as superseded. #148 (Detector-owned draws, PASS/RETURN): there are no draws in the law; a detector clicks or passes by its threshold and window, deterministically; closed. #82 (the generic integer Node processor): the engine of events is that processor (one law, tables, bounded integers; the Beam Law); closed. #149 (the strong interaction residence and binding operator): the strong force in flight on the branch (the sigma column, the lifetime L, the contact through the table, series I) closes it with its pull request. #150 (beta conversion against physical weak decay): the weak force (the transformation `become` under weak-v1, the neutrino's phase width, series J) closes it with its pull request. #151 (the electron's localized recurrence and phase acceptance): series H (Bohr behind the detector: no orbit closed) and the registration of series F answer it; it closes with the hydrogen registration. No physical claim moves by this record.

**RECORDED: series G's "surprise" is not a finding (the physicist's review, 2026-09-20; the owner: "is the surprise a paper?", "can an agent check it?").** The reviewer's verdict, flagged as its own: the "q = -0.55 nearest in 10 of 12 windows" is a limit of the reading and an artefact of the world, not a finding of the law. (1) The pinned criterion, "the nearest of the three forms at the near fit's H", names q = -0.55 even for an exact coasting throw from one point with no offset, because the linear fit through the origin on z at most 0.2 reads the Milne form itself as H t_0 = 1.15; the criterion would have failed on the expected form, and that is the larger cause. (2) The sources' initial offsets pull the near fit's H down and are real physics (each source the Milne form with its own age), but the signature survives their removal; with the exact one-point shift, tau reduced by (r_0 / c) / (1 + z), the coasting readings lie on the Milne form at H t_0 = 1.01 to 1.04 with 0.005 rms in z. (3) The pushing crowd's clock is a gravitational redshift in kind but three to four orders of magnitude above nature's and with a distance gradient nature's sources lack: the world's crowd (one-dimensional beams that do not dilute, a mass at the centre), not the law's cosmology. (4) The ranking is a reproducible bias, worse than noise: the coasting readings separate q = +0.5 from the rest and do not separate q = 0 from q = -0.55 (the forms 0.0055 rms apart against the readings' grain 0.005 to 0.010); the pushing readings separate nothing. (5) Nature's exclusion of the coasting universe came from the z > 1 supernovae and the transition at z about 0.46, then BAO and the CMB; "clocks and an observer at the centre mimic acceleration" is a known family (timescape, LTB voids), and series G's world is a one-dimensional Newtonian version of it. Nothing is publishable: the coasting result is a textbook identity (acoustic Doppler plus flight time from one origin gives Milne's z of tau), and the observable that decides q in nature, the luminosity distance, this world cannot read. Landed with the review: `tools/hubble_readings.py --from-one-point` (the exact shift, labelled GameBoard, printed after the pinned readings), its test, and the follow-up under G in EXPERIMENTS; the registered numbers unchanged. A proper test, recorded for later and not queued: a throw from one point with the criterion validated on the exact expected form before the run (a lesson for the experimenter's skill), H free per form, a luminosity distance from a three-dimensional release against Pantheon+ with errors, z to 1.5 at 0.002 per point, a homogeneous crowd with a weak-field clock, and the acoustic against the relativistic Doppler made explicit (25 percent apart at 0.6 c, where the law and nature part first).

**LANDED: the strong force on the GameBoard, series I, and the wave threshold on the pointer's square (2026-09-20, merge 31cacdf6 on the branch; `python tools/check.py --full` green, 535 tests).** Five commits under the decisions above, each bit-exact against the landed integers where no new key is declared. (1) The columns of the one coupling: per family `"columns": {"<name>": {"value": n or [n, d], "sign": 1 or -1}}`, gravity built in as the universal column [1, 1] with sign minus and never declared, `charge` the shorthand of the column `charge` with sign plus, at most eight columns; the push one signed inner product over the columns per unit of content; verified bit-exact against the landed push form on 49,500 grid cases, 3,000 random cases and the replay of series 7's `read` records, the 66 example worlds' events byte-identical; identity `columns-v1` under `hypotheses` when a column beyond `charge` or a lifetime is declared (the lifetime is the range of a column, the other half of the one mechanism; no third identity). (2) The lifetime, per family `"lifetime": L`, one scalar counting the Links the event has moved, an escape click on the border `lifetime` booked exactly as a face escape; the held content, per measured event `"held": {"<family>": content}`, so the register's proton is `{"family": "p", "amount": 1836, "held": {"nuclear": 1}}` with `nuclear` a phase-less family of column `strong` [10000, 1] sign minus and lifetime 3. (3) The contact through the table: a refused step onto an occupied Node is read by the occupant's table entry for the stepping family, `measure` the hand-over and `rerelease` the bounce; the rule is a function of the resolved table, not of whether an entry was declared (the entry's rule where it differs from the keys' own rule for the family, `measure` otherwise), which keeps the table-from-keys invariant; no key and no identity, a rule of the frame like the no-merge decision, BEAM_LAW note 31; it changes six registered worlds from the first refused step of a body onto a body (coupling 1b at three masses, Bohr r2 and r4, the orbit s8_r12), registered old against new in VALIDATION with notes in the C, D and H entries. (4) Series I, the nucleus (examples/events/nucleus, eight worlds, 21^3 open, 3000 intervals, the 290 fan; 29 readings inside, 1 outside; nothing tuned): the deuteron is bound at one Link and free at three (a square well of depth 3.1 x 10^11 per interval and width two Links, the strong reading the electric reading's own 1 / r^2 with the opposite sign and a cut at the reach, no Yukawa tail; the push on the proton 310,967,280,640 exactly, mirrored on the neutron; no step in 3000 intervals; the label 0 after every hand-over); a kick of 10^12 is outweighed by tick 5; two protons bind or repel by the sign of Q^2 - G^2 - M^2 (the threshold at G = 7111; pp_1 attraction 148,716,220,864 exactly, pp_1_weak repulsion and separation); beyond the reach Coulomb less gravity with no strong read (pp_3's first step at tick 66 the one reading outside the steady toy's 30 to 60, the flight's delays); the model's alpha is the line p n n p and not the square: the square's rows are sheared apart by exactly Q^2 U_d per row, a proton steps at tick 57 and all four bodies are out by tick 538, while the line holds 3000 intervals. He-5, the clock beside the nucleus, the cube of 64 and the lifetimes across fans were cut and said so in the register. Model identities `beam-nucleus-<name>-space-v1`. What the law lacked, none a defect of the column, the lifetime or the contact: a bond that resists shear (central pushes with frictionless contacts make only the collinear cluster rigid; nature's alpha is a tetrahedron), the exclusion principle (a fifth nucleon binds by one bond anywhere), a binding energy in the content (no mass defect; the clock's count under a suspension is the one reading that could carry it), a spread of decay times. The page: https://claude.ai/artifact/L6nLo29cf37FnPzHKLryw5 (the GameBoard drawn, why it was tested, the alpha square dispersing frame by frame, the readings with their kinds, the conclusion). (5) The wave threshold on the pointer's square (#359 step A, #361 fixes 1 and 2): under `wave` a detector set's threshold reads the nearest integer to (X^2 + Y^2) / 2^26, one unit at any phase 1 for every N through 4096, a rays in phase a^2 exactly through a = 11 at N = 64, two opposite 0, so a pair in antiphase passes whether or not a window is declared, no memory between intervals; under `beam` the amount as before; no key, no identity, BEAM_LAW note 32. Of the 66 example worlds 65 are byte-identical, including two_slits, one_slit and the Bell ten (S = 2 exactly before and after: no antiphase pair ever met a pixel of theirs in one interval), so the orchestrator's expectation that their counts would change did not hold; only w27_wave changes (2,312 antiphase pairs pass a pixel at threshold 1 and click later elsewhere, so 38 of its 161 pixels' records differ and the sum of the screen's records moves by 0.16 percent; the count FWHM 0.761 to 0.758, the record FWHM 0.185 and the product 4.99 unchanged), and after the merge the three worlds of A10 at a low rate (every verdict the same, 2 inside 1 outside), all registered old against new; series K unchanged reading by reading. The escaped momentum is booked per family and `run.json`'s `escaped` line per family carries the family's own momentum (#360's minor item).

**RECORDED: the generalized collision's design and the pilot's result (the physicist and the mathematician, 2026-09-20; scratchpad/meeting/MEETING.md, read-only, nothing implemented).** Two findings that decide, flagged as the design's own, for the owner. (1) THE PILOT GIVES NO FRINGES. The two-slit pilot prototype (a free wave in abundance from the openings, rare paid quanta turned at their Node by what they read: the intensity gradient Im(conj(P) F), which one Node holds exactly, or the current Re(conj(P) F), Bohm's direction) counts the quanta's single clicks with no fringes at any rate (1/32, 1/128, 1/512): the correlation of the guided count with the wave's own record fringes (contrast 0.91) is -0.08 and -0.05 for the gradient, -0.20 and +0.20 for the current, where the unguided count reads 0.10, and a third to a half of the current-guided quanta are lost off the screen. The reason is the theorem met before (the mathematician's T4, 2026-09-19): Bohm's guidance is first-order, the direction SET by the wave, a compressing flow, many-to-one; a bijective turn on a fixed-speed unit is second-order, a force, and gives an oscillation about the bright fringe, not Born's density. So on a reversible GameBoard no local pilot carries the single-click fringes, and a local pilot could not carry Bell in any case (a hidden-variable model, S at most 2 by theorem). The honest options for the amplitude (#362) are two, not three: the event is a number, with the single-click build-up and Bell the declared and measured limit (HYPOTHESES 11, the state today), or one object for the pair outside the GameBoard (postulate 22's registry back, "no owner answers at a distance" given up). The decision is the owner's; the orchestrator's recommendation is the first, by the project's own foundations (locality, no draw, no register, the click the only one-way border, the reversible GameBoard). (2) CONSERVATION AND THE SIGN ARE INCOMPATIBLE AT A MEETING. Every unit in transit carries a label of fixed magnitude Q, so exact conservation of momentum at a meeting forces an elastic exchange, and an elastic exchange always kicks the light ALONG the crowd's flow (p . J = -|J|^2 / 2 for every participant): an outward crowd, the field of a mass, repels a conserving light event whatever sign the columns carry, and on a fan the exact exchange exists only between equal weights as the swap of directions (a hard scattering, mean 90 degrees; for light of content 8 against a mass's rays of weight 1, 16 movable joint states of 86,142, in effect nothing). Attraction with the gravity column's sign exists on the GameBoard only as the law's own push written for a beam: a one-sided read, the free crowd untouched, a report and not a balance, exactly as the free push on a body is today. That form (M-R) is a bijection of the joint state (the untouched crowd is back at the Node on the reversed GameBoard, the owner's argument holds for it exactly; the arc permutation of the direction table verified for 1,034 targets on series K's table and 838 on the plane's), restricted to paid units in transit reading the free units of other numbers, the turn counted off the unit's phase register (N crowd units met = one grain step; the age comb fails, measured), content, amount and age untouched; identity `meeting-v1` under a world key `meeting`, absent by default, every registered world byte-identical without it and, since no registered series of bodies has a paid row in transit, bit-identical by the key as well (replayed: paid-meets-free count 0 in nine worlds). Under it series K would read: the sign right (every turn toward the mass), the M / b form of 4 G M / (b c^2) (the crowd met proportional to M / b), the constant the crowd's density over N and not the Newton or Einstein factor, the unit the fan's grain, 10^4 times nature's value at the Sun: the centroid -3.0 pixels for `mass`, -4.3 for `heavy` with 209 rays wrapped to the faces, -2.6 for `near` with 136; no delay beyond the bent path. The conserving form (M-C) was also verified bijective exhaustively (65,536 two-tag slot states) and is repulsive. OWNER'S DECISION NEEDED before any implementation, the design's one recommendation: the sentence "an event in transit reads the crowd as a body does, a report, not a balance" (M-R, and the transit momentum line a report where a beam crosses a crowd), judged on gravity alone; or no meeting at all, series K standing as registered (deflection 0.000). The orchestrator's "momentum conserved, a permutation of the joint state" of the go record above is therefore not one rule but two, and the owner chooses which half.

**DECIDED: the amplitude, option 1 now (the owner, 2026-09-20, "now 1").** An event is a number at one place with a phase; the single-click build-up of fringes and Bell's 2.83 are the declared and measured limit of the law (HYPOTHESES 11), not tuned toward. The pilot route is closed by its own result (no fringes from a bijective turn; T4). Option 3, one record for the pair on the world's side, is not refused: it may come later as a world key with its own model identity (`pair-v1`), absent by default with every registered world byte-identical, and it becomes a claim of the model only if it passes an acceptance test written before it and not built into it (GHZ, three events of one birth; entanglement swapping); until then the model's sentence stays option 1, and a key that gives 2 or 2.83 by a switch predicts nothing about Bell. Nothing on the GameBoard changes by this decision. The owner then asked how the existing engine could support option 3 by blocking a Node for a time so that both detectors feel time 0; the orchestrator's reading and answer are recorded next when it is given.

**LANDED: the four unifications of the formulas (2026-09-20, merge 3a0d4f2e; every logic once, bit-exact: 85 of 85 example worlds byte-identical in events, state and run after each of the four commits; `python tools/check.py --full` green, 539 tests).** (1) The pointer is the first moment of the one reading over the circle: `coherent_pointer` is `read_groups` on the vectors (C[phase], S[phase], 0) with the weights 32 x amount, the separate pointer sum and `POINTER_AMOUNT_BOUND` deleted; beyond the reading's own bound (`reading_fits`) the same table in Python integers, so the record is never refused. (2) Every age against a key is the one `by_clock`: the lifetime's click and the age bound read `ages_at_key(age, key)` = `by_clock(age - 1, 1, key) == 1`; the turn is the release at the rate [n, d] (`NatureBeamWorld.turn`, `turn_rate`), an integer K in the file read as [1, K] with every world bit-identical, a pair accepted like `release`. (3) One moment table over one set object: `DetectorSet.nodes` maps each Node to its event number, a body one event and a declared detector several, `Measured.nodes` read from its set; the presence, the age moment, the record's component and the push's flow come from one `moment_table` per family over the rows at the set with the two masks (present, admitted); the data only, a detector stays a detector. (4) The columns are floored at the clock age like every other rate (note 20's exception closed): no registered series moves (every registered charged product is a multiple of its denominators), three test pins re-pinned with the new integers written first (`test_columns` (b) and (e), `test_nature_beam_push` (k)), and a new test shows an odd product moving every tick by one unit with the cumulative push unchanged. BEAM_LAW note 33, MIGRATION, TEST_EXPECTATIONS, VALIDATION (the replay entry) updated; the glossary rows for `K` and the pointer corrected above (5.6).

**WITHDRAWN: the frozen clock as a route to the pair's correlation (the owner, 2026-09-20, "OK, the clock freeze is cancelled").** The owner had asked how the existing engine could support option 3 by blocking a Node for a time so that both detectors feel time 0. The orchestrator's reading, flagged as its own: the second detector holds the arriving event without a click, its clock frozen (the suspension the engine has), until a return ray from the first click reaches it (the phase after a click and the re-release the engine has), and only then clicks by what the first side read and asked; a local mechanism on the GameBoard, built of existing parts plus one rule (release the held event to a click when the return arrives), and enough for 2.83 since one bit from one side to the other suffices (Toner and Bacon, 2003). Its cost, stated: the return travels at c, so the second click waits a round trip; the detector's own frozen clock does not feel it, but the laboratory's clock does, and the loophole-free experiments timed the two clicks so that no signal at c could pass between them and still read 2.83. So the mechanism would replace "an answer at a distance" by "an answer late", predicting 2.83 only with the wait and 2 without it, against nature's 2.83 without it; honest, reversible, local, and falsified exactly by the timing, by the amount 2 d / c. The owner withdrew it. Option 1 stands as decided above; nothing in the engine changes.

**DECIDED: #363 runs now (the owner, 2026-09-20, "go for it").** Asked what Bell with the choosers on the GameBoard gives: today the counters' windows are numbers in the world file, a hand from outside, so "the model is local" can be answered "your file is local"; with each counter's window read from the phase of a ray arriving from a distant lamp of its own (a third lamp behind Alice, a fourth behind Bob, no shared clock), S = 2 with every E on the triangle would show that the law does not correlate what never met, full determinism without superdeterminism as a measurement, a line no deterministic model of the universe can write about itself; S not 2 would be a mechanism to find. On the GameBoard: three emitters, four detectors, 21 Nodes. Launched to the experimenter with one additive engine key, a table entry's window centre read from the coherent pointer of a named family's rows present at the set in the interval (`"phase_window": {"reads": "<family>", "offset": s}`, the entry passing when none is present, the click carrying the window used), every world without the key byte-identical; the prediction written before the run; the three controls of the issue registered.

**DECIDED: the meeting, M-R (the owner, 2026-09-20, "go for it if it seems generic and smart to you"; the orchestrator's judgement: yes).** The sentence adopted: an event in transit reads the crowd as a body does, a report, not a balance. What it removes is an exception, not a gap: today only a standing event reads the crowd at its Node and answers with a step; under the meeting every paid unit in transit reads, at every free-space Node after the existing table, the free units of every number but its own, by the one reading set and the one coupling (the vector moment V with the labels as weights, the column sum kappa, gravity the universal column so kappa = -1 for a family without columns), and answers with a turn: the target t = kappa V, the direction turned by k steps of the arc permutation of the direction table toward t, k read off the unit's phase register (the crowd met in whole units of Q added to the phase, one grain step per wrap of the circle, so N crowd units met turn the light by one grain, whatever the spacing of the meetings; the age comb was measured to fail); the free crowd untouched, content, amount and age untouched, a phase-less paid family refused under the key. No new constant: the width of the coupling is the crowd's density and N, as the push's is. The books keep a `turned` line per family, a report as `pushed` is; the bijection holds (the inverse recomputes t from the untouched crowd, verified on 1,872 targets); every registered series is bit-identical with or without the key (no paid row in transit in any of them, replayed). The grain is per ray; a beam's centroid at a detector reads the mean of 0, 1, 2 steps, so the value is a detector reading over many rays and not only the sign and the form. What it predicts beyond what it is built for: the light's phase at a detector shifted by the crowd met along its path, an interferometric Shapiro phase, of a size not tuned. What it does not give: a Shapiro delay in time, a horizon, a mass defect, the retarded position and the Nordtvedt-like effect. Identity `meeting-v1` under a world key `meeting` (absent by default, every world byte-identical without it); tests (a) to (f) of the design with their integers first; series K re-registered under the key (expected from the offline flight: `mass` the centroid -3.0 +- 0.5 pixels toward the mass, `heavy` -4.3 with 209 rays wrapped to the faces, `near` -2.6 with 136, the control unchanged), a lensing world registered as a grain, A10 not again. Launched to the implementation.
