**RECORDED: #365, the amplitude as a hub (the other session, 2026-09-20; the owner: "take an issue and think whether it is smart to do it").** The proposal: an event in transit is one record with rows (an amplitude), the collision a norm-preserving integer rotation of the rows, and the click gathers the whole record into one Node chosen from the birth phase against the cumulative |Sigma|^2 over the set, through a hub, a node of the graph with no position on the lattice holding one integer amplitude per row (2^n per n entangled events), one Link from every hosting Node; world key `amplitude`, identity `amplitude-v1`; it would give single-click fringes, Bell's 2.83, GHZ and no-signalling, at the declared cost of the quantum resource. The orchestrator's assessment, flagged as its own: it is quantum mechanics on the lattice, coherent and workable; not now, for three reasons: the hub is the register deleted on 2026-09-17 under a new name (locality on a graph with a hub adjacent to every hosting Node is vacuous; to the issue's own question, it is a register); it reverses four foundations recorded today (option 1, no register, fixed local storage, no owner answers at a distance); and it adds nothing beyond quantum mechanics, the lattice carrying geometry only, so the model's sentence would become "quantum mechanics on an integer lattice" in place of a claim that can fail. Kept as the keyed alternative, absent by default, with the issue's five acceptance tests (Mach-Zehnder always one port; two slits at a low rate; HOM; A2 with the choosers at 2.83 with the marginals 1/2; hydrogen's levels) written before any run, to be taken only if the owner wants the sentence to change; the warning that the Link version read 2.875 at N = 64, above Tsirelson's 2.828, to be settled before any claim. The decision is the owner's; nothing in flight depends on it.

## 5.5 Acceptance tests and open decisions

Every supported operation needs declared initial ownership, input, operator, expected output and model time, conserved readouts, edge cases and failure conditions. Accepted principles are not reopened by missing code or tests. Numerical family rules and Detector distributions need explicit closure where still undefined. The once-only encounter requirement needs independent tick traces and implementation evidence. A stated acceptance requirement is not a passing test.

**How tests are written and run (model owner, 2026-09-17).** The engine is generic and its rules are separated, so a test exercises one generic rule in isolation on a minimal board and nothing else: one test module per rule, one per feature of the ray-event model, with the expected integers written down before the first run. No test pins the numbers of an example world, compares two worlds or reproduces a known experiment; those are research runs (section 6), made once, recorded with a fingerprint and a date, and never repeated as tests. A change is checked only against the tests that depend on what it changed, selected by the import graph; the whole suite runs together only when the shared core changes (the Node and its Ports, the order of the cycle, the bounded integers, the phase), and then once, in parallel. A physical milestone, a phenomenon that several rules produce together, is one run of the engine with a fingerprint and a dated record, not a suite. The cost of checking is proportional to the risk of the change, never constant. (2026-09-19: the tests of the engine of events are listed below, "The engine of the law of events: what the tests show".)

**Open decision: what a returned field quantum does after spreading (2026-09-17).** A returning ray retraces its line by its step count (section 5.4); once the field spreads (feature 12) a field quantum's path is no longer one line, so a quantum that a Detector returns with 0 cannot walk back to its source that way, and a field ray has no event at which to perform an inverse split. The orchestrator's proposal, to be decided by the model owner before experiment A1: the returned quantum reverses on the line it arrived by and walks back until it is absorbed by the first content its coupling responds to or reaches its source, with no inverse split; every audit stays exact since nothing is created. Until decided, feature 12 implements exactly that and says so. Settled on 2026-09-18 by the law of the bit (section 5.4, points 3 and 6): a returned shadow turns back with its heading reversed and its momentum inverted and mixes at every Node like any shadow; there is no walk and no inverse split of a shadow, and it is absorbed wherever it reaches its owner. Settled again on 2026-09-18 (the evening) by the law of the shadow (section 5.4): there is no return; a quantum that reaches held content is absorbed, and a mark returns nothing. (2026-09-19, the law of events: closed, nothing returns; "So we do not want a return to the source.")

**The constants: what is derived and what is an input (2026-09-17).** Mass ratios are derivable: the ladder of hypothesis 12 is the set of loop-closing contents under the catalog's binding table, and experiment A10 counts them against the known spectrum, the proton-to-electron ratio first. The strength of the electric coupling and the strength of gravity are inputs today: the release ratio n/d of a field family and the modulus N of the lag register (section 3.28) are declared widths, and hypothesis 14's G = ħc/(N m₀)² is a reparametrization until something fixes N. The Born table's ratios were an input of the same kind until 2026-09-18, when the model owner ruled that the table is computed from the phase width N (section 5.4, point 17). Two open hypotheses record the question, 16 (what fixes the lag modulus: the top of the mass ladder, the resolution the spreading field needs, or nothing) and 17 (what fixes the release ratio and the table: the symmetry of section 3.27 and the path counting of 3.5, or nothing); until they are answered the model predicts forms, 1/N² and 1/r², and not the values of G and α. (2026-09-18: the field family is retired, point 12, the ratio being the size of a family's shadow set, "A thing does not emit"; the lag register is retired with point 22; the Born table is computed from N, point 17; what fixes G remains hypothesis 16's question.) (2026-09-18, the evening, the law of the shadow: the size of a shadow set became a rate with "A thing emits", ρ, the release per unit of held content per interval, the orchestrator's name, and it is the input for the strength of a force, the model owner's to choose; what fixes G remains hypothesis 16's question.) (Under the law of events, 2026-09-19: the inputs are the declared widths of the world file of 5.4, ρ, K, N, the suspension's width, the exchange rate of a paid family and the detectors' sensitivities; nothing else is free.)

**Open decisions of the law of the shadow (2026-09-18, the evening; section 5.4).** What round 8 of DERIVATIONS.md must show before the new engine (feature 20, field-only-v1) is trusted, none of it assumed: that a held content is stable, its gravity and electricity free and its light paid; that its step, a release toward the neighbour and an absorption there, keeps it at or below the field's speed; that a content in uniform motion absorbs no net momentum from its own field, which is inertia; that bound contents exchanging quanta give the ladder of hypothesis 12; and what a split and a pair creation are as tables. Open with them, the orchestrator's list, flagged: what a mark that misses does, what the steering of two things of one family becomes, what a decay is as a table on held content, and what the siblings of one event are when nothing walks a path. Not open: Bell, two events of one field at two places, each born of the local field and the local setting, at most 2 (reading (vii)). The value of ρ, the rate of release, is the model owner's, as the size of the shadow set was. (2026-09-19, the evening, the model owner: "No confrontations are needed. Only tests that everything is as designed." The engine is the one engine without any of the above shown by a run; what round 8 was to show stays a list of research questions for runs made when wanted and registered in EXPERIMENTS.md, not a condition of anything; what round 8 did show on paper is in DERIVATIONS.md sections 51 to 56. The two choices decided as declared widths the same day, the phase turn in flight and the quantum of a family, are in 5.4's status line.) (2026-09-19, the law of events: recorded in 5.4 after "One speed, and what is seen" and implemented the same evening as `events-v1`, the one engine, the engine of the law of the shadow deleted; what it changes is listed there and in Highlights coverage, and what shows it is tests of the code, not runs.)

**The engine of the law of events: what the tests show (the orchestrator's list, 2026-09-19, flagged; implemented the same evening as `events-v1`, the modules named in [Highlights coverage](HIGHLIGHTS_IMPLEMENTATION.md)).** Tests of the code on minimal boards, no runs: (a) everything that enters a Node in an interval leaves it or is measured in the same interval, nothing kept at the Node; (b) a single quantum leaves whole to one side, its momentum's heading, wherever it is and whatever else is there of another number; (c) nine coherent units of one number entering on one Port leave four back and one on each other heading, and a large bundle within five units of the shares; (d) a suspended event counts down on itself, and the event behind it on the lane leaves after it, never before; (e) the books, in transit, measured and escaped, and the momentum over all records, balanced at every interval; (f) a measured event of content below the release's denominator still releases, at its rate off its clock, and a family's phase in transit advances its declared steps per Link; (g) the world file's refusals by name; (h) the pinned integers of the slit worlds re-pinned from the new engine, as a check that it does what the law says and not as a result.

## 5.6 The glossary of the names and how each is computed (2026-09-20)

The model owner, 2026-09-20: "the architect is to make sure the names of the system are uniform, that none is redundant, and that each has a definition in Highlights of how it is computed." The architect's table follows, read from the code with every formula in integers as the code forms it; the last part lists the redundancies with the one name recommended, which the orchestrator adopts as the naming plan (executed with the NatureBeam rename). A name's row is the definition; the law's owner document stays RAY_LAW.

Architecture review, 2026-09-20, for the model owner's glossary. Read from the
code at `1884b8b5` (branch `claude/universe24-new-3ytqde`; the detector as a
set with one record merged at 00:06 UTC) with the formulas checked line by
line at `f88443f2` and re-read where the merge changed them. Every formula is
in integers as the code forms it; `//` is the floor division, `isqrt` the
integer root, `mod` the remainder. "Same name" says whether the code, the
tests, the tools, the examples and the live documents (RAY_LAW, ENGINE,
TERMINOLOGY, the READMEs) use one name; the aliases found are listed and the
recommended single name is in the last section. Bounds: every amount, content,
momentum component, K and tick is refused above 2^62 - 1 (`AMOUNT_BOUND`,
`MOMENTUM_BOUND`); a charge and a quantum above 2^30 - 1 (`MAX_VALUE`).

### World-file keys (`events/world.py`, `parse_ray_world`)

| Name | Computed / read in | Definition (integers) | Defined in | Same name; aliases |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `law` | `parse_ray_world` | the string `"rays"` selects the one engine; the record carries `RAYS_LAW` = `"rays-v1"` | RAY_LAW 1; ENGINE "The world" | yes; `LAW_VALUE` (code constant) |
| `model_id` | `parse_ray_world` | a nonempty string, the world's identity | ENGINE "The world" | no: `run.json` writes it as `model` |
| `shape` | `parse_ray_world` | three extents, each 1 .. 4096; the flat index of a Node is `x (Y Z) + y Z + z` (`RayStore.flat`) | RAY_LAW 2 | yes; `extents` (local) |
| `boundary` | `_boundary` | `"open"` or `{x,y,z: "open"|"periodic"}`, missing axes open; `periodic[axis]` true wraps the walk (`coordinates[:, axis] mod extent`); an open face is the face detector `face:+x` .. `face:-z` | ENGINE "Per-axis board topology" | yes; `periodic` (parsed tuple), `boundary_per_axis` (preflight) |
| `ticks` | `parse_ray_world`; `execute_ray_run` | the intervals to run, 0 .. 2^62 - 1; `tick` is the interval's index from 1 | ENGINE "The record" | no: "tick" (code, records, tools) and "interval" (documents, Highlights) name the same step |
| `K` | `parse_nature_beam_world` -> `NatureBeamWorld.K` (as declared) and `turn_rate` = (n, d) | the turn as the release at the rate [n, d] phase steps per unit of content per self-creation: `s = NatureBeamWorld.turn(clock_age, content) = by_clock(clock_age, content x n, d)`; an integer K in the file is the pair [1, K], bit-identical (the four unifications, 2026-09-20) | BEAM_LAW 2, 3 step 5, note 33; ENGINE "A release costs" | one name: `K` in the file and the record, `turn_rate` the pair it resolves to |
| `N` | -> `RayWorld.phase_steps` | the steps of the phase circle, a power of two 2 .. 4096, 64 by default; `phase_mask = N - 1` | RAY_LAW 2 | no: `N`, `phase_steps` (world), `modulus` (`nature_beam` local), `phase_mask`; `Measured.phase_steps` is a different quantity (the cumulative turn) |

| rule `read` | step 4 | the push taken (below), the rays go on; a `read` record with the push and the reading | RAY_LAW 3 step 4 | yes |
| rule `measure` | step 4 | the click: the rows leave the store, `held[family] += amount x content`, `events[family] += amount`, the labels join the momentum (paid: push = V), the set's record grows | RAY_LAW 3 step 4, 5 | yes; "click" (the event and the record kind) |
| rule `rerelease` | step 4, 5 | the rows leave the store into `pending[family]` as (amount, content, phase); at the next self-creation each is apportioned whole over `directions`, age 0, the re-emitter's number, the re-emitter's (charge, held[family]) | RAY_LAW 5 | yes; "re-emission", "mirror", "opening" (prose), the deleted `transduce` |
| rule `pass` | step 4 | the rows are not met and leave no record; they count in the presence | RAY_LAW 3 step 4 | yes; a `pass` RECORD is something else (a ray below the threshold or outside the window) |
| `phase_window` (s) | `ray_tables` (`window`), step 4 | a step 0 .. N - 1; a phase p is inside iff `d = (p - s) mod N` has `4 d < N` or `4 d >= 3 N`; on a table entry the response is made only to a ray inside it (under `wave`, to the set's phase); on a lamp a release only when the clock phase is inside | RAY_LAW 3 step 4; ENGINE "The world" | no: `phase_window` (key), `window` (code, records), "width" (TERMINOLOGY) |
| `reads` | `_table_entry`; `Reading.component` | one of `scalar`, `outside`, `here`, `vector`, `tensor`: the component the record's `reading` field carries; `vector` by default on `read`, `scalar` otherwise; no rule changes with it | RAY_LAW note 10, 16 | no: `reads` (key), `reading` (the record field AND the detector key), `Reading` (class), `Readings` (dense diagnostics), `read` (rule), `read_arrivals` |
| `lamp` `{rate, directions, phase_window}` | step 5 | at a self-creation of turn s > 0 (and clock phase inside the window, if any), per direction `amount = min(by_clock(clock_age, rate_n, rate_d), held // (h s))`, content per unit `h s`, `held -= h s x amount`; recoil `-sum(labels born)` | RAY_LAW 3 step 5; ENGINE "A release costs" | yes; "source", "sun" (prose) |
| `in_transit[]` | `RaySimulation.__init__` | a declared row: position, family, number (1 .. max(1, measured)), `direction` (a vector of D or a rest index), amount, phase, `age` (reduced mod L_d today); content `h`; a free ray takes (charge, declared amount) of its number's event, (0, 0) without one | RAY_LAW 2, note 12, 20 | no: `heading` (deleted, refused) |
| `detectors[].name` | `_detectors` | a nonempty unique label; the parser accepts a name in `FACE_NAMES` (a collision in the record) | ENGINE | yes |
| `detectors[].positions` | `_detectors`; `DetectorSet.numbers` | Nodes of measured events, each in at most one detector; the set with ONE record; a measured event outside every detector is a set of one | ENGINE; Highlights 5.4 | yes; "coverage", "width" (prose) |
| `detectors[].threshold` | step 4 | 1 by default; the arrivals of every number but each Node's own, summed over the set in the interval, below it pass with a `pass` record (`threshold`) | RAY_LAW 5 | yes |
| `detectors[].reading` | `_detectors`; step 4 | `"beam"` (default: the arriving rays paired by opposite phase over the set, a pair passes on, the rest click, the record the count clicked) or `"wave"` (the record the square of the coherent pointer; the window reads the set's phase; the set's phase returned to its measured events) | ENGINE "The world" (1884b8b5); Highlights 5.4 | landed after this review began; homonym of `reads`/`reading` field |
| `age_bound` (in flight) | not in the code | the bound of a whole age on a periodic board (Highlights) | Highlights 5.4 | not yet |

### The ray's record (`NatureBeam`; the store's columns, all int64)

| Name | Computed in | Definition | Defined in | Same name; aliases |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `node` | walk; `RayStore.flat` | the flat index `x (Y Z) + y Z + z`; after a step `coordinates + step_d(age)`, wrapped on a periodic axis; through an open face the row leaves (a face click) | RAY_LAW 2, 3 step 1 | no: `position` (x, y, z) in `NatureBeam`, `state.json` and the measured event |
| `direction` | collision only | an index of D; the label's and the flight's direction; changed only by the collision table (a single unit in a slot) | RAY_LAW 2, 4 | no: "heading" for the six unit vectors (indices 2 .. 7, `PORT_HEADINGS`); `arrival` (the direction the row arrived on this interval, `HERE` = 0 if it did not step) |
| `age` | walk | the flight phase: `(age + 1) mod L_d` at every interval for a moving direction; kept for a rest direction; 0 at birth; in flight: kept whole, the flight reading `age mod L_d` | RAY_LAW 2, 3, note 2 | no: `Measured.age` is the clock (self-creations); `clock_age` its value before the frame's advance |
| `phase` | walk | `(phase + phase_per_link) mod N` at every Link crossed; stamped by the emitter at birth; kept by a re-emission | RAY_LAW 2 | yes |
| `number` | birth | the last emitter's number; a re-emission stamps the re-emitter's | RAY_LAW 2 | yes; "owner" (`RayWorld.owners`), "emitter" (prose) |
| `amount` | merge | whole units; identical rows are one row with the amounts added (`merge`, bounded) | RAY_LAW 2, note 3 | no: "units" (ledger `face_units`, `escaped_units`, prose "in units") |
| `content` | birth | the content one unit carries: `h x s` for a paid ray, 0 for a free one; an identity field of the merge | RAY_LAW 2 | no: "carried" (`t_carried`, `content_absorbed`: amount x content), `held`, `mass` |
| `charge` | birth (step 5) | a free family's ray: the emitter's charge at birth; 0 on a paid ray | RAY_LAW 2, note 20 | yes |
| `mass` | birth (step 5) | a free family's ray: the emitter's `held[family]` at birth (M_B, the electric divisor); 0 on a paid ray; 0 also on a re-emitted ray of a family the re-emitter holds none of (finding B2) | RAY_LAW 2, note 20 | no: the quantity is a content (`held`, `amount`, "M_B"); the column is called `mass` |

| the set's phase | `pointer_phases` | the step k with `X C[k] + Y S[k] > 0` minimizing `|X S[k] - Y C[k]|`, the lowest k on a tie; None for a zero pointer; under `beam` the last clicked row's phase (1884b8b5, `last_phase`) | ENGINE (1884b8b5); Highlights "the phase returned" | landed after this review began |
| the age moment (in flight) | not in the code | `sum amount x age` over the arrivals of the reading set, `reads: "age"` | Highlights 5.4 | not yet |

### Tables and functions that hold a rule

| Name | Where | Definition | Defined in | Same name; aliases |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Q | `nature_beam.Q` | 64, the flight table's resolution (and, in flight, the label's scale) | RAY_LAW 3 | yes |
| S_1 | `flight_table` -> `manhattan` | `|a| + |b| + |c|` of a direction | RAY_LAW 3 | no: `manhattan` (code) |
| T_d | `flight_table` -> `turns` | `isqrt(3 (a^2 + b^2 + c^2) Q^2)`; T_d >= S_1 Q | RAY_LAW 3 | no: the array is named `turns`, a homonym of the clock's `turn` |
| m(tau) | `FlightTable.manhattan_steps` | `(2 tau S_1 Q + T_d) // (2 T_d)`; `m(tau + 1) - m(tau)` in {0, 1} | RAY_LAW 3 | yes |
| line_d | `_bresenham` -> `lines` | S_1 unit steps: at step j the axis maximizing `|v_i| (j + 1) - S_1 |position_i|`, the lowest axis first | RAY_LAW 3 | yes; "the digital line" |
| step_d(tau) | `flight_table` -> `steps` (int8) | `line_d[m(tau) mod S_1]` if `m(tau + 1) > m(tau)`, else (0, 0, 0); the inverse subtracts `step_d(tau - 1)` | RAY_LAW 3 | yes; "the walk" |
| L_d | `flight_table` -> `period` | `p = T_d // gcd(S_1 Q, T_d)`; `per = S_1 Q p // T_d`; `L_d = p x (S_1 // gcd(per, S_1))`; 1 for a rest direction | RAY_LAW 3 | no: `period` (code) |
| the collision table | `collision_table` | states `s in {0, 1, 2}^8` (empty, single = one row of amount 1, crowd), code `sum s_k 3^k`; class `(crowd mask, n singles, S = sum of the singles' headings)`; members sorted as 8-tuples; forward = the cyclic shift +1, inverse -1; applied per (Node, number, content) class to rows with `direction < 8` at a Node without a measured event; a moved unit takes its new slot's direction by rank | RAY_LAW 4, note 18 | yes; "slot" = heading index 0 .. 5 or rest 6, 7 (`slot = 6 + direction` for a rest row, `direction - 2` for a heading) |
| `label_weights` | `nature_beam` | `amount` (free) or `content x amount` (paid), refused if `max|amount| x max|content| > 2^62 - 1` | RAY_LAW 2 | yes |
| `momentum_labels` | `nature_beam` | `label_weights x D[direction]` per row (in int64; the product is not bounded before it is formed: finding B1) | RAY_LAW 2, note 18 | no: "label", "momentum", the record key `push` on a click (a label), `momentum` on a face click and a step |
| the push (`push_form`) | inline in `nature_beam` step 4 (no function of that name exists) | per (reader A, group of number B): `V = sum label` over the group's rows; free ray: `push = -V x content_A` plus, if `charge_A != 0`, per (q_B, M_B) class `t = V_class x q_A x q_B` (bounded), `push += sign(t) x by_clock(age_A, |t|, M_B)`; paid ray: `push = V`; `momentum_A += push`, `pushed_A += push` (each bounded) | RAY_LAW 3 step 4, note 20 | no: `push_form` is named in RAY_LAW, ENGINE, TERMINOLOGY, MIGRATION and HIGHLIGHTS_IMPLEMENTATION but no such function exists; "push", "push taken", "pushed", "recoil" |
| `by_clock(age, n, d)` | `core/integer.py` | `((age + 1) n) // d - (age n) // d`, d >= 1; exact on average, no remainder kept | RAY_LAW 3; ENGINE | yes; "off the clock", "rate", "the whole part"; a re-export in `engine.by_clock` |
| `apportion_whole(total, weights, first)` | `core/integer.py` | `share_k = total w_k // sum w`; the `total - sum share` units left go to the largest remainders, ties from index `first` in cyclic index order | RAY_LAW 3 step 5 | yes |
| the turn s | `engine._frame_all` | `by_clock(clock_age, content, K)`; refused if `2 s >= N`; the phase then `(phase + s) mod N` | ENGINE "The frame" | no: `turn` (code) vs `FlightTable.turns` (T_d); `Measured.phase_steps` (the cumulative turn) vs `RayWorld.phase_steps` (N) |
| the clock's count | `engine._suspend` | `owed = by_clock(clock_age, presence x n, d)`; paid one per interval; no self-creation, release, turn or step while owed > 0; `waited` counts the intervals | ENGINE | no: "owed", "count", "suspension" |
| the presence | `family_plan` | `sum amount` of every row at the reader's Node of another number, every family, rest and moving alike; `Measured.presence` | RAY_LAW 3 step 2 | no: "scalar" |
| the step | `engine._move` | above (`width`); `adjacent_node` gives the target; a target with a measured event refuses (the step counted); an open face: the event leaves (a face click with its `held` and `pending`) | ENGINE | yes; "refused step" |
| the threshold | `family_plan` | over the set's arrivals of other numbers, `sum amount < threshold` -> every row passes with a `pass` record naming `threshold` | RAY_LAW 5 | yes |
| the window | `family_plan` | per row (or per set under `wave`): `tables.window[(phase - s) mod N]`; outside -> a `pass` record naming `window` | RAY_LAW 3 step 4, note 5 | yes |
| the detector's record | `family_plan`, the record loop | `wave`: `record += X^2 + Y^2` per set per family per interval (Python integers); `beam`: `record += the count clicked` after the pairing | RAY_LAW 5, note 19; ENGINE (1884b8b5) | no: "record", "squared record", "coherent record"; the `record` line's `record` field |
| C[k], S[k] | `core/phase.py` | fixed-point cosine and sine of `2 pi k / N` in 256ths from an integer series (`_PI_FIXED` 3141592654 / 10^9), cached per N; `C^2 + S^2 <= 65897 < 257^2` | RAY_LAW 5 | yes; "the 1/256 tables" |
| `exact_sum`, `exact_column_sums`, `grouped_sums` | `nature_beam` | an int64 reduction taken in the register when `max|v| x count <= 2^62 - 1`, in Python integers otherwise | note 19, 22 | yes |
| `bounded(value, entry, quantity)` | `nature_beam` | refuses `|value| > 2^62 - 1` naming the measured event, its Node and the quantity | ENGINE "The books" | yes |
| `merge_key` | `RayStore` | the identity fields (node, direction, age, phase, number, content, charge, mass) packed as `key = (key << width_k) + (column - min)` when `sum width_k <= 62`, else the lexsort of the same fields | note 22 | yes; "the packed key" |

### The run's record

| Name | Written by | Keys | Defined in | Same name; aliases |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `run.json` | `execute_ray_run` | `package_version`, `source_sha256`, `initialization_sha256`, `law`, `model`, `shape`, `boundary`, `K`, `N`, `release`, `suspension`, `width`, `directions` (the table beyond the rest and the headings), `families[{name, quantum, charge, phase, phase_per_link}]`, `numbers{n: {position, family}}`, `status`, `error`, `requested_ticks`, `completed_ticks`, `tick`, `elapsed_seconds`, `conserved_at_every_completed_tick`, `audit[books per tick]`, `measured_content[]`, `transit_content[]`, `momentum[]`, `measured[Measured.state()]`, `detectors[]` (name, nodes, threshold, reading, families{measured, clicks, record, phase}; then the faces: measured, clicks, content, record, measured_content; momentum), `escaped[{family, amount, content, momentum}]`, `display`, `initialization_resolution` | ENGINE "The record" | `model` vs `model_id`; `escaped[].momentum` is the total over families, repeated per family |
| `Measured.state()` | `measured.py` | `number, position, family (index), held, content, phase, charge, momentum, fixed, windows, detector (index), age, owed, home, home_content, waited, phase_steps, steps, measured{home, read, measure, rerelease}, events, pushed` (`record` moved to the set at 1884b8b5) | ENGINE | `events` holds the clicks; `family`/`detector` are indices where the records elsewhere carry names |
| `state.json` | `snapshot_writer` from `snapshot_stream` | `law, tick, shape, boundary, measured[], detectors[], escaped[], nodes[{position, families[{family (name), rays[{direction (vector), age, phase, number, amount, content, (charge, mass)}]}]}]` | ENGINE "The record" | `position` vs `node`; `direction` a vector here, an index in the store |
| `events.jsonl` kinds | `nature_beam`, `engine._move` | `home` (tick, node, measured, detector, family, number, amount, push = the labels in, content); `pass` (…, amount, phase, window, threshold); `read` and `rerelease` (…, number = the other, amount, push, content, reading); `click` at a detector (…, amount, push = the row's label, phase, content, reading); `click` on a face (node, measured None or the event's number, detector `face:*`, family, number, amount, phase, momentum, content; for an event also held, home, home_content); `record` (tick, node/measured or None for a set of several, detector, family, number 0, record, pointer under `wave`, phase); `step` (tick, number, node, to, momentum) | ENGINE "The record" | `push` on a click is a label, `momentum` on a face click is the same label; `measured` on a line is the reader's number, `number` the emitter's |

### Redundancies and inconsistencies, with the one name recommended

1. **presence / scalar / count** — one quantity, three names (`Reading.scalar`, `Measured.presence`, `Readings.presence`; `Readings.count` is `outside`; "count" also names `owed` and the `beam` record). Recommend: `presence` for the scalar everywhere (`reads: "presence"`), `arrived` for `outside`, `owed` for the clock, `clicks` for the beam record.
2. **flow / vector** — `Reading.vector`, `Readings.flow`, "net flow", "label moment". Recommend: `flow` (`reads: "flow"`), and `label_flow` for V.
3. **pointer / amplitude / coherent sum / coherent reading / the wave** — one (X, Y). Recommend: `pointer` (with `amplitude` for the row's `32 x amount` only).
4. **here / rest** — `here` is the reading of rows that did not step (rest rays and dwelling rays); a rest direction is index 0 or 1; `HERE = 0` reuses the rest index as the arrival code. Recommend: `here` for the reading, `rest` for the two directions, a distinct constant `NO_ARRIVAL` for the code.
5. **suspension / owed / the count / the clock's count** — recommend: `suspension` for the key, `owed` for the state and the prose.
6. **label / momentum / push** — the row's label is written as `push` on a detector click and as `momentum` on a face click and a step; `push` should mean a push taken. Recommend: `momentum` on every record line that carries a label; `push` only in `read`/`rerelease`/`home` (what the reader took).
7. **kind / quantum / KINDS** — `kind` is deleted for families; `configuration_validation.KINDS` is the preflight's kind of document. Recommend: rename `KINDS` to `DOCUMENT_KINDS` or drop it (one kind).
8. **heading / direction** — keep `direction` for a ray's field and the table; `heading` only for the six unit vectors (`PORT_HEADINGS`, the collision's slots).
9. **tick / interval** — the code and the records say `tick`; the documents and Highlights say "interval". Recommend: `tick` in every record key and identifier, "interval" as the prose noun defined once in TERMINOLOGY as "the step tick t -> t + 1".
10. **turn / turns / phase_steps** — `FlightTable.turns` (T_d) is a homonym of the clock's `turn`; `Measured.phase_steps` (the cumulative turn) is a homonym of `RayWorld.phase_steps` (N). Recommend: `FlightTable.resolution` for T_d, `Measured.turned` for the cumulative turn, `N` kept as `phase_steps`.
11. **K / clock** — resolved on 2026-09-20: the key `K` is `NatureBeamWorld.K` as declared and resolves to the pair `turn_rate`; the turn is the release at that rate.
12. **width** — the push's `width`, the phase window's "width" (TERMINOLOGY), the detector set's "width" (Highlights), the suspension's "fractional width". Recommend: `width` for the push only; "window" for `phase_window`; "extent" for a set's Nodes; "rate" for `suspension`.
13. **amount / content / held / mass / carried / measured_content** — `amount` is units for a ray and content for a measured event; `mass` on a ray is the emitter's `held`; `carried` is `amount x content`. Recommend: `content` for a measured event's key (in place of `amount`), `emitter_content` for the column `mass`, `carried` documented once as `amount x content`.
14. **units / amount** — `face_units`, `escaped_units`, "in units". Recommend: `amount`.
15. **events / clicks** — `Measured.events` holds the clicks (`run.json` says `clicks`). Recommend: `clicks`.
16. **node / position** — `node` on event lines and in the store, `position` in `state.json`, the world and `Measured`. Recommend: `node` for the triple in every record (TERMINOLOGY: the Node is the location).
17. **model / model_id** — `run.json` writes `model`. Recommend: `model_id`.
18. **measured** — the run's list, the ledger line, the tally by rule and the reader's number on an event line. Recommend: `reader` for the event line's field (its partner `number` -> `emitter`), `taken` for the tally, `measured` for the line and the list.
19. **RULES vs TABLES** — `measured.RULES` (`home, read, measure, rerelease`, the tally keys) is not the rule set (`world.TABLES`: `read, measure, rerelease, pass`). Recommend: `TALLIES`.
20. **reads / reading / Reading / Readings / read** — five near-homonyms. Recommend: `reads` (the component key), `reading` (the detector's law, `beam`/`wave`), `Moments` for the class `Reading`, `BoardDiagnostics` for `Readings`, and the record field `reading` -> `component`.
21. **push_form** — five documents name a function that does not exist; the push is computed inline in `nature_beam` step 4. Recommend: either extract `push_form(...)` as the documents say (one place, the same integers) or correct the five documents to "the push of step 4 in `nature_beam`".
22. **Leftovers of the deleted engines in live names**: `test_repository_hygiene.duplicate_files(ignore_run_duration)` keys on `disturbance_types`; `OLD_KEYS` (23 refused keys) and `EVENTS_KEYS`, `REVERSIBLE_KEYS` are refusal tables (keep, they name the law in the refusal); `ui.py`'s renderer CSP and `.html` branch (audit A9); the `events` package name (kept by the design: a ray is an event in transit); `Sec-Fetch-Site` is an HTTP header, not the deleted noun.
23. **`face:*` as a user detector name** — accepted by the parser, two `face:+x` in the record. Recommend: refuse a detector name in `FACE_NAMES`.

# 6. History, implementation and evidence

The Evidence and references tab in the central specification collects code anchors and scoped previous results. Immutable event and wave-origin history is distinct from a Node's bounded active references and does not authorize remote history reads. Cancellation must follow the selected branch causally without deleting past records, unrelated paths or spatial topology. Exact bounded lookup, stopping, conflicting-notice and future-packet handling still require closure. A return undoes a share of an event on the board; it does not rewrite the recorded history, and no event is moved.

Git documents and source code describe particular software interfaces and experimental profiles; each result applies only to its identified source revision and tested scope. Existing generic-contact or capture samplers do not by themselves satisfy the Detector-only rule of section 3.19. Older shared-clock profiles and an incomplete six-output-clock candidate do not establish the complete adopted engine contract. Candidate annexes support implementation review but cannot silently replace adopted definitions. Bell results recorded with the former shared resource (section 3.18, deleted) are historical and are not evidence for the current model, and PASS/RETURN values carried on returning rays do not by themselves establish entanglement or spacelike Bell correlations. This documentation review adds no simulator execution or new passing result.

# 7. Working method

Only developers write or modify runtime code and tests. The architect coordinates interfaces; physics and mathematics own meanings and operators; experimental review fixes independent expectations; documentation maintains definitions and evidence status. These roles do not imply continuously running agents.

# 8. Documentation integrity check

The central specification owns adopted model decisions, the defined Detector and Node/Link acceptance, with open items identified explicitly. This document summarizes that owner rather than maintaining competing detailed laws. Review ordinary locality without exception, Detector-only sampling at marked Nodes, six-output timing and exact ownership. Distinguish unresolved numerical choices, engineering gaps and unproved emergence from already adopted principles. A candidate, unsupported profile or historical result must never silently override an adopted definition.

# 9. Assistant working instruction

Do not suggest a next step or follow-up action unless Alon explicitly asks for it.
